&=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{E}^{j}\left(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}-(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\right)-\mathbf{F}^{j}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t,\end{split}

(6.53)

where the auxiliary matrices
\mathbf{E}^{j}\in\mathbb{C}^{l\times l}
and
\mathbf{F}^{j}\in\mathbb{C}^{l\times k}
are the
(1,1)
and
(1,2)
blocks of
\mathbf{C}_{j+1}^{j}
, respectively, as shown in (
6.47
), and are explicitly given up to leading-order
O(\Delta t)
by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{E}^{j}&:=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}\Delta t)\left[\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)^{\hskip 3.27773pt-1}_{11}+\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)^{\hskip 3.27773pt-1}_{11}\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)_{12}\mathbf{D}^{j}\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)_{12}^{\hskip 3.27773pt\mathtt{T}}\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)^{\hskip 3.27773pt-1}_{11}\right]\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{D}^{j}\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)_{12}^{\hskip 3.27773pt\mathtt{T}}\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)^{\hskip 3.27773pt-1}_{11}\Delta t\\
&=\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}),\end{split}

(6.54)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{F}^{j}&:=-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}\Delta t)\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)^{\hskip 3.27773pt-1}_{11}\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)_{12}\mathbf{D}^{j}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{D}^{j}\Delta t\\
&=-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Big[(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}+\left((\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{K}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}-\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big)^{-1}(\mathbf{H}^{j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}+(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}\right)\Delta t\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\left(\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}+\mathbf{K}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}\Delta t\right)\Big]+O(\Delta t^{2}),\end{split}

(6.55)

with
\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}\in\mathbb{C}^{l\times l}
defined in (
6.31
),
\left(\mathbf{C}_{22}^{j}\right)_{11}\in\mathbb{C}^{l\times l}
and
\left(\mathbf{C}_{22}^{j}\right)^{\hskip 3.27773pt\mathtt{T}}_{12}\in\mathbb{C}^{l\times k}
given in (
6.39
), and
\mathbf{D}^{j}\in\mathbb{C}^{k\times k}
provided in (
6.41
), and are expressed up to first-order terms as

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{j}_{j}:=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}\Delta t)\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)^{\hskip 3.27773pt-1}_{11}=\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}-\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}),

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)_{11}=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\left(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\mathbf{H}^{j}\Delta t\right),\quad\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)^{\hskip 3.27773pt\mathtt{T}}_{12}=\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t,

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}^{j}=\left(\frac{1}{\Delta t}\mathbf{I}_{k\times k}+\mathbf{L}^{j}-(\mathbf{L}^{j})^{2}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})\right)\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}

\displaystyle\mspace{110mu}=\frac{1}{\Delta t}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}+\mathbf{K}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}+\mathbf{L}^{j}\mathbf{K}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}),

where the auxiliary matrices
\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\in\mathbb{C}^{k\times l}
,
\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j}\in\mathbb{C}^{l\times l}
,
\mathbf{H}^{j}\in\mathbb{C}^{l\times l}
,
\mathbf{L}^{j}\in\mathbb{C}^{k\times k}
,
\mathbf{K}^{j}\in\mathbb{C}^{k\times l}
are defined as

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}:=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big)^{-1},\quad\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j}:=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big)^{-1},

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}^{j}:=(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}})^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}^{j}:=\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j},\quad\mathbf{L}^{j}:=\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}=\mathbf{K}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}.

Finally, by definition we have
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{J,J}=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{J}
and
\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{J,J}=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{J}
, i.e., the smoother Gaussian statistics coincide with the filter ones on the end point
t_{J}=T
.

Proof of Lemma
6.7
.

Note that although all discrete estimates we have seen thus far do depend on
J
(and by extend on
\Delta t
), since they are evaluated on
t_{j}=j\frac{T}{J}
(e.g.,
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}
,
\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}
,
\mathbf{m}_{j}^{j}
,
\mathbf{P}_{j}^{j}
, etc.), for the Gaussian statistics of the discrete smoother this dependence is noted explicitly since we condition over the whole observation period, and as such on all
J+1
observations of
\mathbf{x}
;
\mathbf{x}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{x}^{J}
.

Note that we have already showed the desired expressions for
\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}
,
\left(\mathbf{C}_{22}^{j}\right)_{11}
,
\left(\mathbf{C}_{22}^{j}\right)^{\hskip 3.27773pt\mathtt{T}}_{12}
, and
\mathbf{D}^{j}
in the proof of Lemma
6.6
, in (
6.42
), (
6.43
), (
6.44
), and (
6.45
), respectively. We have also recovered the expressions of the block parts of
\mathbf{C}^{j}_{j+1}
,
\mathbf{E}^{j}
and
\mathbf{F}^{j}
, up to
O(\Delta t)
in the absence of noise cross-interaction (i.e., when
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\equiv\mathbf{0}_{l\times k}
), in (
6.48
) and (
6.49
). As such, we just need to prove the discrete smoother formulae (
6.52
)–(
6.53
), and the form of the block matrices (
6.54
)–(
6.55
) for the general case (as well as to cross-check to see that when we assume absence of noise cross-interaction we recover the expressions we already have).

Having already established in the proof of Lemma
6.6
the auxiliary expressions required here, we can now fully determine the blocks of
\mathbf{C}_{j+1}^{j}
,
\mathbf{E}^{j}
and
\mathbf{F}^{j}
, up to leading-order terms, for the general case where noise cross-interaction could be present. Specifically, using (
6.42
)–(
6.46
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{E}^{j}&:=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}\Delta t)\left[\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)^{\hskip 3.27773pt-1}_{11}+\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)^{\hskip 3.27773pt-1}_{11}\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)_{12}\mathbf{D}^{j}\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)_{12}^{\hskip 3.27773pt\mathtt{T}}\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)^{\hskip 3.27773pt-1}_{11}\right]\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{D}^{j}\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)_{12}^{\hskip 3.27773pt\mathtt{T}}\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)^{\hskip 3.27773pt-1}_{11}\Delta t\\
&=\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}+(\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger})\mathbf{K}^{j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})\\
&=\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}+((\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}-\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger})\mathbf{K}^{j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})\\
&=\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}-\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{K}^{j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})\\
&=\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}})^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\mathbf{K}^{j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})\\
&=\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}),\end{split}

(6.56)

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}^{j}

\displaystyle:=-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}\Delta t)\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)^{\hskip 3.27773pt-1}_{11}\left(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}\right)_{12}\mathbf{D}^{j}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{D}^{j}\Delta t

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}\Delta t)\big((\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}+\big((\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{K}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}-\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big)^{-1}(\mathbf{H}^{j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}\big)\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta t}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}+\mathbf{K}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}+O(\Delta t)\right)\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Big((\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}

(6.57)

\displaystyle\quad+\big((\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{K}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}-\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big)^{-1}(\mathbf{H}^{j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}+(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{K}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}\big)\Delta t\Big)+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Big[(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}+\big((\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{K}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}-\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big)^{-1}(\mathbf{H}^{j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}+(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}\big)\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big(\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}+\mathbf{K}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}\Delta t\big)\Big]+O(\Delta t^{2}).

Notice how in the absence of cross-interaction noise (i.e.,
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\equiv\mathbf{0}_{l\times k})
,
\mathbf{E}^{j}
reduces down to
\mathbf{C}^{j}_{j}+O(\Delta t^{2})
, as already shown in (
6.48
). As for
\mathbf{F}^{j}
, observe that when
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\equiv\mathbf{0}_{l\times k}
then we have
\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}
and
\mathbf{K}^{j}=\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}
. With these reductions we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}^{j}

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Big[(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}+\big((\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{K}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}-\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big)^{-1}(\mathbf{H}^{j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}+(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}\big)\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big(\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}+\mathbf{K}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}\Delta t\big)\Big]+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Big[(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}+(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{K}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}\Delta t-\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big)^{-1}(\mathbf{H}^{j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}\Delta t+(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{K}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}\Delta t\Big]+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=\big((\mathbf{H}^{j})^{\dagger}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}\big)(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=\big(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}\big)(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}),

which is exactly the expression that we had in (
6.49
).

Having established the expressions for all auxiliary matrices, we can now finally proceed with the inspection of the conditional joint distribution of
p(\mathbf{y}^{j},\mathbf{y}^{j+1}|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J)
and the recovery of the discrete smoother formulae for the posterior (smoother) mean and covariance. By definition of the conditional distribution, we have

\displaystyle p(\mathbf{y}^{j},\mathbf{y}^{j+1}|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J)

\displaystyle=p(\mathbf{y}^{j}|\mathbf{y}^{j+1},\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J)p(\mathbf{y}^{j+1}|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J)

\displaystyle=p(\mathbf{y}^{j}|\mathbf{y}^{j+1},\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq j+1)p(\mathbf{y}^{j+1}|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J)\ (\because\text{ Markov property}).

The first conditional on the right is Gaussian, as shown in Lemma
6.6
,
\mathbb{P}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j}\big|\mathbf{y}^{j+1},\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq j+1\big)\overset{\mathrm{d}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}_{l}(\mathbf{m}^{j}_{j+1},\mathbf{P}^{j}_{j+1})
, with the conditional mean and covariance given by (
6.29
)–(
6.30
). As for the second conditional, after marginalizing the discrete-time result of Theorem
2.1
through Lemma
6.2
, we have that it also follows a normal distribution, which we denote by
\mathcal{N}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J},\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J})
. As such, by marginalizing this equation through Lemma
6.3
(over
\mathbf{y}^{j+1}
) and by using (
6.29
)–(
6.30
), we arrive at the following result (where again note that we are conditioning on
\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J
):

\displaystyle p(\mathbf{y}^{j}|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{C}^{l}}p(\mathbf{y}^{j},\mathbf{y}^{j+1}|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}^{j+1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{C}^{l}}p(\mathbf{y}^{j}|\mathbf{y}^{j+1},\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq j+1)p(\mathbf{y}^{j+1}|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}^{j+1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{C}^{l}}\mathcal{N}_{l}(\mathbf{y}^{j};\mathbf{E}^{j}\mathbf{y}^{j+1}+\mathbf{b}^{j},\mathbf{P}^{j}_{j+1})\mathcal{N}_{l}(\mathbf{y}^{j+1};\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J},\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J})\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}^{j+1},\ \mathbf{b}^{j}:=\mathbf{m}^{j}_{j+1}-\mathbf{E}^{j}\mathbf{y}^{j+1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}_{l}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j};\mathbf{E}^{j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}+\mathbf{b}^{j},\mathbf{E}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}+\mathbf{P}^{j}_{j+1}\big).

The explicit expression of
\mathbf{b}^{j}
is

\mathbf{b}^{j}=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{E}^{j}\big((\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t\big)+\mathbf{F}^{j}\big(\mathbf{x}^{j+1}-\mathbf{x}^{j}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{x},j})\Delta t\big),

(6.58)

where
\mathbf{E}^{j}
and
\mathbf{F}^{j}
up to order
O(\Delta t)
are given in (
6.56
)–(
6.57
). As a result, with the help of (
6.29
)–(
6.30
) and the previous expressions, we see that the mean and covariance of the discrete smoother are

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j,J}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}^{j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}+\mathbf{b}^{j}=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{C}_{j+1}^{j}\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t\\
\mathbf{x}^{j+1}-\mathbf{x}^{j}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{x},j})\Delta t\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{E}^{j}\big(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t\big)+\mathbf{F}^{j}\big(\mathbf{x}^{j+1}-\mathbf{x}^{j}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{x},j})\Delta t\big),

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j,J}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}^{j}_{j+1}+\mathbf{E}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{C}_{j+1}^{j}\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}(\mathbf{C}_{j+1}^{j})^{\dagger}+\mathbf{E}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{E}^{j}(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{F}^{j}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t+\mathbf{E}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{E}^{j}\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}-(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big)-\mathbf{F}^{j}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t,

since it is easy to see that

\mathbf{C}_{j+1}^{j}\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}(\mathbf{C}_{j+1}^{j})^{\dagger}=\mathbf{C}_{j+1}^{j}(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{12})^{\dagger}=\mathbf{E}^{j}(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{F}^{j}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t,

(6.59)

where we note that this recovers the previous expression we had in (
6.51
), when we have assumed the absence of noise cross-interaction. This finishes the proof of Lemma
6.7
.
∎

With these Lemmas, we are ready to prove the optimal nonlinear smoother state estimation backward equations.

Proof of Theorem
2.3
.

This is Theorem 12.10 in Liptser & Shiryaev
[
69
]
. For the analogous result in the case of discrete time, see Theorem 13.12, which outlines the corresponding optimal recursive nonlinear smoother backward difference equations, with the respective sufficient assumptions given in Subchapters 13.2.1 and 13.3.8. Here we instead provide a martingale-free proof by working over the time discretization of our model and then transitioning to the continuous-time case via the usual limit.

Recall from Lemma
6.7
, that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j,J}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{E}^{j}\big(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t\big)+\mathbf{F}^{j}\big(\mathbf{x}^{j+1}-\mathbf{x}^{j}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{x},j})\Delta t\big),

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j,J}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{E}^{j}\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}-(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big)-\mathbf{F}^{j}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t,

for every
j=0,1,\ldots,J
, where the auxiliary matrices appearing in these expressions are provided up to leading-order terms in Lemma
6.7
and its proof.

We start off with the smoother mean. Subtracting
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}
on both sides of (
6.52
) yields

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j,J}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\mathbf{E}^{j}\big(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t\big)+\mathbf{F}^{j}\big(\mathbf{x}^{j+1}-\mathbf{x}^{j}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{x},j})\Delta t\big).

We now try to account for all the terms on the right-hand side of this mean-difference equation. Recalling the expressions for
\mathbf{E}^{j}
and
\mathbf{F}^{j}
proved in Lemma
6.7
and by letting
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j,J}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}=\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1}+\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2}+\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{3}+\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{4}
, for
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}
’s to be determined based on the expansions of these auxiliary matrices, then for each term, by suppressing terms of order higher that
O(\Delta t)
, we have:

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1}
:

Letting
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1}:=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}+\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}\big(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t\big)
, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}+(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}-\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\big(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t\big)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}+\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t+\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J})\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=(-\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y},j}+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}))\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}).

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2}
:

Expanding terms, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}^{j}\big(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}\big(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t\big)

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\big(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t\big)\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}),

where we used the fact that
\mathbf{E}^{j}=\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})
. The first term is already accounted for in
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1}
, and so we let

\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2}:=(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\big(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t\big)\Delta t.

We then have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2}

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big)^{-1})(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J})\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J})\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}).

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{\gamma}_{3}
:

Letting
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{3}:=-\mathbf{F}^{j}\big(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{x},j}\big)\Delta t
, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{3}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big((\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\big)\big(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{x},j}\big)\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big((\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}+(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big)\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\big(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{x},j}\big)\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\big(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{x},j}\big)\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}).

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{\gamma}_{4}
:

First, we let
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{4}:=\mathbf{F}^{j}\left(\mathbf{x}^{j+1}-\mathbf{x}^{j}\right)
. But, observe from (
2.10
) that

\Delta\mathbf{x}^{j}:=\mathbf{x}^{j+1}-\mathbf{x}^{j}=\left(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\mathbf{y}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y},j}\right)\Delta t+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{\mathbf{y},j}\sqrt{\Delta t}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{1}^{j}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{2}^{\mathbf{y},j}\sqrt{\Delta t}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{2}^{j}=O(\sqrt{\Delta t}),

and by linearity
\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\mathbf{x}^{j}\big|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J\right]=O(\Delta t)
. As such, in a similar manner as with
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{3}
, we see that

\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{4}=-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\left(\mathbf{x}^{j+1}-\mathbf{x}^{j}\right)+O(\Delta t^{3/2}).

Adding all of these together based on the definitions of the
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}
’s and expansions of the auxiliary matrices as shown in Lemma
6.7
, and carrying out the necessary algebra, after taking the limit as
\Delta t\to 0^{+}
, or equivalently as
J\to\infty
, we end up with

\overleftarrow{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}}(t)=-\big(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y}}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y}}-\mathbf{B}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}})\big)\mathrm{d}t+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{-1}\big(\overleftarrow{\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}}+(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x}}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{x}})\mathrm{d}t\big),

for
T\geq t\geq 0
and where the
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}
matrices are given as in (
6.14
)–(
6.15
). The backward-arrow notation on the left-hand side, and
\overleftarrow{\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}}
on right-hand side, denotes

\overleftarrow{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}}(t):=\lim_{\Delta t\to 0}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}(t)-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}(t+\Delta t)\right),\quad\overleftarrow{\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}}(t):=\lim_{\Delta t\to 0}\left(\mathbf{x}(t)-\mathbf{x}(t+\Delta t)\right),

for
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}(t)
being the conditional mean of the smoother distribution
\mathbb{P}\big(\mathbf{y}(t)\big|\mathbf{x}(s),s\in[0,T]\big)
at time
t
, where on the right-hand side the smoother Gaussian mean is evaluated at
t+\Delta t
, while all other terms and the filter mean are being evaluated at
t
.

We now look into the smoother covariance tensor. Subtracting
\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j,J}
on both sides of (
6.53
) yields

\displaystyle\hskip-34.14322pt\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j,J}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-\mathbf{C}_{j+1}^{j}\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}(\mathbf{C}_{j+1}^{j})^{\dagger}+\mathbf{E}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-\mathbf{E}^{j}\left(\mathbf{C}_{22}^{j}\right)_{11}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}-\mathbf{F}^{j}\left(\mathbf{C}_{22}^{j}\right)^{\hskip 3.27773pt\mathtt{T}}_{12}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\hskip 66.86414pt-\mathbf{E}^{j}\left(\mathbf{C}_{22}^{j}\right)_{12}(\mathbf{F}^{j})^{\dagger}-\mathbf{F}^{j}\left(\mathbf{C}_{22}^{j}\right)_{22}(\mathbf{F}^{j})^{\dagger}+\mathbf{E}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger},

where we have used (
6.39
), (
6.47
), and block-matrix algebra to expand the term
\mathbf{C}_{j+1}^{j}\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}(\mathbf{C}_{j+1}^{j})^{\dagger}
. We now try to account for all the terms on the right-hand side of the covariance-difference equation by letting

\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j,J}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}=\sum_{i=1}^{6}\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i},

for
\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i}
’s to be determined based on the expansions of auxiliary matrices up to leading-order terms as outlined in Lemmas
6.6
and
6.7
. Specifically, for each term, by suppressing terms of order higher than
O(\Delta t)
, we have:

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{\Theta}_{1}
:

Letting
\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{1}:=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}+\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}\Big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-\left(\mathbf{C}_{22}^{j}\right)_{11}\big)(\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j})^{\dagger}
, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}+(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}-\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\mathbf{H}^{j}\Delta t)\big)(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}-\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)^{\dagger}+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=-\big(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\big)\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}).

\bullet\Bigg\{\begin{subarray}{c}\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{2}\\
\\
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{3}\end{subarray}
:

Expanding terms, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}^{j}\Big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-\left(\mathbf{C}_{22}^{j}\right)_{11}\Big)(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}\Big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-\left(\mathbf{C}_{22}^{j}\right)_{11}\Big)(\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle+\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big)(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t

\displaystyle+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big)(\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j})^{\dagger}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}),

since
\left(\mathbf{C}_{22}^{j}\right)_{11}=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+O(\Delta t)
and
\mathbf{E}^{j}=\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})
. The first term is already accounted for in
\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{1}
, and so we let

\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{2}=\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big)(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t,

and
\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{3}=(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{2})^{\dagger}
. Using then the fact that
\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}=\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+O(\Delta t)
, it is immediate that

\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{2}=\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big)(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}),

and likewise for
\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{3}=(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{2})^{\dagger}=(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big)\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})
.

\bullet\Bigg\{\begin{subarray}{c}\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{4}\\
\\
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{5}\end{subarray}
:

Letting
\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{4}:=-\mathbf{F}^{j}\left(\mathbf{C}_{22}^{j}\right)^{\hskip 3.27773pt\mathtt{T}}_{12}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}
, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{4}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}((\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}+O(\Delta t))\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}((\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j})^{\dagger}+O(\Delta t))\Delta t

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}((\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger})\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j})^{\dagger}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}),

and likewise for

\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{5}:=(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{4})^{\dagger}=-\mathbf{E}^{j}\left(\mathbf{C}_{22}^{j}\right)_{12}(\mathbf{F}^{j})^{\dagger}=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}).

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{\Theta}_{6}
:

Letting
\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{6}:=-\mathbf{F}^{j}\left(\mathbf{C}_{22}^{j}\right)_{22}(\mathbf{F}^{j})^{\dagger}
, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{6}

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}((\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}+O(\Delta t))\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}-\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}+O(\Delta t))\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}((\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger})\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}-\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}).

Like we did for the smoother mean, adding all of these together, and based on the definitions of the
\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i}
’s and expansions of the auxiliary matrices as shown in Lemmas
6.6
and
6.7
, carrying out the necessary algebra and afterwards taking the limit as
\Delta t\to 0^{+}
, or equivalently as
J\to\infty
, yields

\overleftarrow{\mathrm{d}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}}(t)=-\big((\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{B}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{-1})\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}(\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{B}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{-1})^{\dagger}-\mathbf{B}\big)\mathrm{d}t,

for
T\geq t\geq 0
, where the
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}
matrices are given as in (
6.14
)–(
6.15
), and with the backward-arrow notation on the left-hand side denoting

\overleftarrow{\mathrm{d}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}}(t):=\lim_{\Delta t\to 0}\left(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}(t)-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}(t+\Delta t)\right),

for
\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}(t)
being the conditional covariance of the smoother distribution
\mathbb{P}\big(\mathbf{y}(t)\big|\mathbf{x}(s),s\in[0,T]\big)
at time
t
, where on the right-hand side the smoother covariance is evaluated at
t+\Delta t
, while all other terms and the filter estimate are being evaluated at
t
.

Finally, the existence and uniqueness of continuous solutions to (
2.15
)–(
2.16
), that continuously depend on the initial distribution and additional model parameters, can be established by the fact that
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}(t)
and
\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}(t)
solve backward random ordinary differential equation that are linear in
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}(t)
and
\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}(t)
, respectively, with matrix-valued coefficients and forcings that satisfy the conditions of the random variant of the Picard-Lindelöf theorem (under our assumed regularity conditions)
[
148
,
151
,
149
]
; adaptations are of course needed from the parallel approach in establishing these properties for the filter posterior Gaussian statistics, since (
2.15
)–(
2.16
) now run backwards in time (with a terminating instead of an initial condition, specifically the identification of the smoother statistics through the filter ones)
[
169
,
200
,
201
]
. An alternative approach to the existence, uniqueness, and continuity of the smoother Gaussian statistics is provided in the corresponding results from Liptser & Shiryayev
[
69
]
.
∎

6.4
Proof of Theorem
3.1

Before proceeding with the derivation and proof of the formula for conditional optimal nonlinear forward sampling of the unobserved process in a CGNS, we first establish the following lemma.

Lemma 6.8 (Cross-Covariance of
\boldsymbol{(\mathbf{y}^{j},\mathbf{y}^{j+1})}
Conditioned on
\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J
).

The cross-covariance matrix between
\mathbf{y}^{j}
and
\mathbf{y}^{j+1}
when conditioned on the whole observed time series of
\mathbf{x}^{s}
,
s\leq J
, for
j+1\leq J
, is given by

\mathrm{Cov}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j},\mathbf{y}^{j+1}\big|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J\big)=\mathbf{E}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J},

where
\mathbf{E}^{j}
is defined by (
6.55
).

Proof of Lemma
6.8
.

The standard proof would start from writing down the details of the joint distribution of
\mathbf{y}^{j}
and
\mathbf{y}^{j+1}
conditioned on the observations
\mathbf{x}^{s}
,
s\leq J
, granted
j+1\leq J
. Here, we instead utilize a shortcut proxy method to arrive at the form of this cross-covariance matrix. A detailed proof which derives the form of this cross-covariance matrix by solving for the joint Gaussian distribution in detail, in the absence of cross-interacting noise feedbacks, can be found in Theorem A.2 of Chen
[
202
]
(i.e., for
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\equiv\mathbf{0}_{l\times k}
); the general case is an easy adaptation of that approach, by using the full
2\times 2
block covariance matrix instead of a diagonal one.

We know from the discrete-time variant of Theorem
2.1
, that the following joint distribution is Gaussian:

\mathbb{P}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j},\mathbf{y}^{j+1}\big|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J\big)\overset{\mathrm{d}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}_{2l}\left(\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j,J}\\
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j,J}&\mathrm{Cov}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j},\mathbf{y}^{j+1}\big|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J\big)\\
\mathrm{Cov}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j+1},\mathbf{y}^{j}\big|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J\big)&\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}\end{pmatrix}\right).

Then, in light of Lemma
6.4
, the conditional mean of
\mathbf{y}^{j}
given
\mathbf{y}^{j+1}
and
\mathbf{x}^{s}
,
s\leq J
, is given by

\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{y}^{j}\big|\mathbf{y}^{j+1},\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J\right]=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j,J}+\mathrm{Cov}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j},\mathbf{y}^{j+1}\big|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J\big)\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}\big)^{-1}(\mathbf{y}^{j+1}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}).

But, in the general case where noise cross-interaction is present, this mean is exactly given by
\mathbf{m}_{j+1}^{j}
in (
6.29
), due to the Markovian property illustrated in (
6.38
). As such, by matching the terms appearing in front of
\mathbf{y}^{j+1}
, after recalling that
\mathbf{C}_{j+1}^{j}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{E}^{j}&\hskip 3.99994pt\mathbf{F}^{j}\end{pmatrix}
), it is immediate that
\mathrm{Cov}\left(\mathbf{y}^{j},\mathbf{y}^{j+1}\big|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J\right)=\mathbf{E}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,J}
.
∎

We now proceed with the algorithm for forward sampling of the hidden process conditioned on the current observations.

Proof of Theorem
3.1
.

By the discrete-time variant of Theorem
2.1
,
\mathbb{P}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j+1},\mathbf{y}^{j}\big|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq j+1\big)
is normal (after using the exchangeability (in law) property of jointly distributed Gaussian random vectors by virtue of Lemma
6.2
and permutation matrices). Likewise, by
\mathbb{P}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j},\mathbf{x}^{j+1}\big|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq j\big)=\mathbb{P}\big(\mathbf{x}^{j+1}\big|\mathbf{y}^{j},\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq j\big)\mathbb{P}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j}\big|,\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq j\big)
and Lemma
6.3
, as a result of (
2.10
), Lemma
6.2
, and Theorem
2.2
, so is
\mathbb{P}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j},\mathbf{x}^{j+1}\big|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq j\big)
. Also, note that in discrete time, for the forward sampling procedure, observations are given up to the
(j+1)
-st time instant which we consider to be the end point of the observable time series, and as such only at this point, by definition, the smoother and filter posterior Gaussian statistics coincide, i.e.,

\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,j+1}=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}\text{ and }\mathbf{R}_{\text{{s}}}^{j+1,j+1}=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}.

(6.60)

This observation is utilized in the sequel.

Now, we turn our attention to the joint distribution
p(\mathbf{y}^{j},\mathbf{x}^{j+1}|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq j)
. As mentioned, this distribution is Gaussian (
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.), and by (
6.5
) and the optimal nonlinear filter estimate, it is explicitly expressed as

\mathbb{P}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j},\mathbf{x}^{j+1}|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq j\big)\overset{\mathrm{d}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}_{l+k}\left(\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\\
\mathbf{x}^{j}+(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{x},j})\Delta t\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}&\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\Delta t\\
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t&(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\Delta t\end{pmatrix}\right).

As such, by virtue of Lemma
6.4
, we have that
\mathbb{P}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j}\big|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq j+1\big)\overset{\mathrm{d}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}_{l}(\mathbf{m}_{\text{f}}^{j,j+1},\mathbf{P}_{\text{f}}^{j,j+1})
, where the first superscript denotes the time step we are currently on for the unobserved process, while the latter denotes up to which time step we condition on, and the subscript “
f
” denotes that this is a forward- or filter-based distribution. Note how we also condition on
\mathbf{x}^{j+1}
now, and as such this distribution differs from the filter posterior distribution. If we now define
\mathbf{M}^{j}:=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\mathtt{T}}
, then

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{m}_{\text{f}}^{j,j+1}&=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{M}^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\big(\mathbf{x}^{j+1}-\mathbf{x}^{j}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{x},j})\Delta t\big)=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+O\big(\sqrt{\Delta t}\big),\\
\mathbf{P}_{\text{f}}^{j,j+1}&=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{M}^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+O(\Delta t).\end{split}

(6.61)

While at the end point,
t_{j+1}
, we have that the filtering and smoothing Gaussian statistics coincide, at
j
we see that this is not necessarily the case. But at order
O(1)
, the filtered distribution is also recovered for the
j
-th time step. This observation is utilized in what follows.

In a very similar manner, and by recalling the observation in (
6.60
) and the normality of
\mathbb{P}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j+1},\mathbf{y}^{j}\big|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq j+1\big)
, we have

\mathbb{P}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j+1},\mathbf{y}^{j}\big|\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq j+1\big)\overset{\mathrm{d}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}_{2l}\left(\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}\\
\mathbf{m}_{\text{f}}^{j,j+1}\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}&\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}\\
\mathbf{E}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}&\mathbf{P}_{\text{f}}^{j,j+1}\end{pmatrix}\right),

where the form of the cross-covariance matrix comes from Lemma
6.8
for
J=j+1
. Then Lemma
6.4
once again gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{y}^{j+1}|\mathbf{y}^{j},\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq j+1\right]:=\mathbf{m}_{\text{f}}^{j+1,j+1}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}(\mathbf{P}_{\text{f}}^{j,j+1})^{-1}(\mathbf{y}^{j}-\mathbf{m}_{\text{f}}^{j,j+1}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j+1}\big|\mathbf{y}^{j},\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq j+1\big):=\mathbf{P}_{\text{f}}^{j+1,j+1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}(\mathbf{P}_{\text{f}}^{j,j+1})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}.

We now express these Gaussian statistics up to leading-order terms. But before that, it is important to observe that our end-goal is to produce a forward sampling formula, meaning our focus is extracting a discrete equation for the difference
\mathbf{m}_{\text{f}}^{j+1,j+1}-\mathbf{y}^{j}
up to leading-order
O(\Delta t)
, and then passing to the limit as to retrieve an SDE that can be used to forward sample the unobserved process based on the observations up until
\mathbf{x}^{j+1}
. But exactly because our focus is the difference
\mathbf{m}_{\text{f}}^{j+1,j+1}-\mathbf{y}^{j}
, and not
\mathbf{y}^{j+1}-\mathbf{y}^{j}
, it stands to reason that the statistics at point
j
, for arbitrarily small
\Delta t
, should necessarily coincide with the corresponding Gaussian filtering statistics. As such, we essentially have that

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{\text{f}}^{j+1,j+1}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})^{-1}(\mathbf{y}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\text{f}}^{j+1,j+1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1},

for
\Delta t\ll 1
, where we note the appearance of
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}
and
\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}
on the right-hand side instead of
\mathbf{m}_{\text{f}}^{j,j+1}
and
\mathbf{P}_{\text{f}}^{j,j+1}
, respectively; this can also be argued rigorously through (
6.61
).

As such, by using the results from Lemma
6.7
, where we showed that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}^{j}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}-\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j}\big)\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}),

we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{\text{f}}^{j+1,j+1}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})^{-1}(\mathbf{y}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})^{-1}(\mathbf{y}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})

\displaystyle\hskip 48.36958pt+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}\big((\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}-(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}\big)(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})^{-1}(\mathbf{y}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}).

We now substitute
\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+(\mathbf{N}_{1}^{j}+\mathbf{N}_{2}^{j})\Delta t
back into the previous equation, where by the discretization of (
2.13
), (
6.11
)),
\mathbf{N}_{1}^{j}
and
\mathbf{N}_{2}^{j}
are defined by

\displaystyle\mathbf{N}_{1}^{j}

\displaystyle:=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\mathbf{H}^{j},

\displaystyle\mathbf{N}_{2}^{j}

\displaystyle:=-\big(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\big(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\big)

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j},

with the auxiliary matrices
\mathbf{H}^{j}
and
\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}
given in Lemma
6.7
. Doing so, we end up with

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{\text{f}}^{j+1,j+1}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}+(\mathbf{y}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})+(\mathbf{N}_{1}^{j}+\mathbf{N}_{2}^{j})(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})^{-1}(\mathbf{y}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 48.36958pt+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big((\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}-(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}\big)(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})^{-1}(\mathbf{y}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}),

or equivalently, based on the definitions of
\mathbf{N}_{1}^{j}
,
\mathbf{N}_{2}^{j}
,
\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}
, and
\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbf{m}_{\text{f}}^{j+1,j+1}-\mathbf{y}^{j}=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}(\mathbf{y}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})\Delta t+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})^{-1}(\mathbf{y}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})\Delta t\\
&\hskip 2.84544pt-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}(\mathbf{y}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})\Delta t\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})^{-1}(\mathbf{y}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})\Delta t\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})^{-1}(\mathbf{y}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})\Delta t-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})^{-1}(\mathbf{y}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})\\
\\
&\hskip 65.44142pt\equiv\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+(\mathbf{A}^{j}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{j})(\mathbf{y}^{j}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}).\end{split}

(6.62)

This exactly accounts for the drift term in (
3.1
) when passing to the limit. We just need to get a hold of the diffusion coefficient as well.

Recall from elementary SDE theory that for an Itô diffusion process with noise coefficient
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
, the covariance matrix for its infinitesimal increments is exactly given by
\boldsymbol{\sigma}\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\dagger}\Delta t
. As such, accounting for the self-adjointness, the noise coefficient for the forward sampling formula is the square root of the positive-definite matrix
\mathrm{Var}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j+1}|\mathbf{y}^{j},\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq j+1\big)=\mathbf{P}_{\text{f}}^{j+1,j+1}
. We now proceed to find the form of this matrix up to
O(\Delta t)
and take its unique square root. Going along this path, and by again using
\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+(\mathbf{N}_{1}^{j}+\mathbf{N}_{2}^{j})\Delta t
and the expression of
\mathbf{E}^{j}
from before, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\text{f}}^{j+1,j+1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}(\mathbf{E}^{j})^{\dagger}(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}\Big[\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}-\left(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+((\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}-(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger})\Delta t\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\times(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})^{-1}\big(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}-\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j})\Delta t\big)(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+(\mathbf{N}_{1}^{j}+\mathbf{N}_{2}^{j})\Delta t)\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j+1}\Big[\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}-\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}-(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})^{-1}((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}-\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j})\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-(\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})^{-1}(\mathbf{N}_{1}^{j}+\mathbf{N}_{2}^{j})\Delta t-((\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}-(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger})\Delta t\Big]+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j}-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t-(\mathbf{N}_{1}^{j}+\mathbf{N}_{2}^{j})\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}((\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}-(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j})\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}\Delta t+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 21.33955pt-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})^{\dagger}\Delta t-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-2(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 21.33955pt+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle\equiv(\mathbf{B}^{j}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}).

Since
\mathbf{P}_{\text{f}}^{j+1,j+1}=(\mathbf{B}^{j}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j})\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})
, this establishes that
\mathbf{B}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}
is nonnegative-definite
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., or at least the infimum over time of its smallest eigenvalue is nonnegative
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., where (
3.3
) can also be seen by the fact that
\mathbf{B}\succeq\mathbf{0}_{l\times}

\mathbb{P}
-a.s., as shown in the proof of Theorem
2.2
, and by the assumption on the positive-definiteness of the filter covariance matrices and
(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{-1}
over time. As such its unique square root exists. So, by collecting the results thus far, using (
6.62
), and taking the limit
\Delta t\to 0^{+}
, leads to

\mathrm{d}\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}=\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}+(\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma})(\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}})\mathrm{d}t+(\mathbf{B}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}})^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{W}_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}},

which is exactly what we wanted to prove.
∎

6.5
Proof of Theorem
3.2

Proof of Theorem
3.2
.

In this proof, the overline notation denotes taking the expectation of the process in question conditioned on the sub-
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}(t)}
. We plug-in the mean-fluctuation decomposition (
6.1
) into (
3.1
), or equivalently by linearity, take the ensemble average of (
3.1
) conditioned on
\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}(t)}
, to get

\mathrm{d}\overline{\hat{\mathbf{y}}}_{\text{f}}=\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}+(\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma})(\overline{\hat{\mathbf{y}}}_{\text{f}}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}})\mathrm{d}t,

(6.63)

where
\mathbb{E}\left[\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}(t)\Big|\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}(t)}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{y}(t)|\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{x}}_{t}\right]\Big|\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}(t)}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{y}(t)\big|\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathbf{x}}\right]=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}(t)
by the stability property of conditional expectations and since
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{y}(t)\big|\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathbf{x}}\right]
is a
\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathbf{x}}
-measurable process with
\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathbf{x}}\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}(t)}
.

Now, if
\overline{\hat{\mathbf{y}}}_{\text{f}}
at the initial time instant equals the filtering mean,
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}
, then
\overline{\hat{\mathbf{y}}}_{\text{f}}\equiv\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}
at all time instants, due to the uniqueness of the solution to (
6.63
) by the underlying assumptions considered thus far, regardless of whether we are talking about its weak or strong solution. On the other hand, if
\overline{\hat{\mathbf{y}}}_{\text{f}}
differs from the filter mean
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}
at the initial time instant, i.e., in the case of misidentified initial conditions, then
\overline{\hat{\mathbf{y}}}_{\text{f}}\to\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}
, as time evolves, instead, with this convergence being exponentially fast. This is an immediate consequence of the assumption that the real parts of all the eigenvalues of
\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}
are negative (
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.) and by the linearity of (
6.63
)
[
149
,
150
,
156
,
157
,
158
,
151
]
, where the exponential convergence is due to the mean-square exponential stability of the filter posterior mean which follows again by this condition on the spectrum of
\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}
. This establishes
(a)
.

Now, by subtracting (
6.63
) from (
3.1
), yields an SDE for the fluctuation part of
\hat{\mathbf{y}}
,

\mathrm{d}\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime}=(\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}t+(\mathbf{B}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}})^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{W}_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}}.

(6.64)

The covariance is then given by
\overline{\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime})^{\dagger}}
, which by Itô’s lemma
[
122
,
124
,
125
,
163
,
203
]
and linearity, has a differential given by

\mathrm{d}\left(\overline{\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime})^{\dagger}}\right)=\overline{\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime}(\mathrm{d}\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime})^{\dagger}}+\overline{\mathrm{d}\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime})^{\dagger}}+\overline{\mathrm{d}\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime}(\mathrm{d}\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime})^{\dagger}}.

(6.65)

As such, by plugging-in (
6.64
) into (
6.65
), and using the facts

\displaystyle\overline{\mathrm{d}\mathbf{W}_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime})^{\dagger}}=\overline{\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{W}_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}})^{\dagger}}=\mathbf{0}_{l\times l},

\displaystyle\overline{\mathrm{d}\mathbf{W}_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{W}_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}})^{\dagger}}=\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}\mathrm{d}t,

then up to leading-order term
O(\mathrm{d}t)
we have,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\left(\overline{\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime})^{\dagger}}\right)

\displaystyle=\big((\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\overline{\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime})^{\dagger}}+\overline{\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime})^{\dagger}}(\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}-\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}})+\mathbf{B}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}\big)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\big(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}+\mathbf{B}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}\big)\mathrm{d}t,

which is exactly the same evolution equation as the for the filter covariance (
2.13
) after using the definitions of
\mathbf{A}
,
\mathbf{B}
, and
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}
((
6.14
), (
6.15
), and (
3.2
), respectively), with the last equality being a result of the fact that either
\overline{\hat{\mathbf{y}}}_{\text{f}}=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}
or
\overline{\hat{\mathbf{y}}}_{\text{f}}\to\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}
exponentially fast in time, depending on the initial conditions, and as such, by continuity,
\overline{\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime})^{\mathtt{T}}}=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}
or
\overline{\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{\text{{f}}}^{\prime})^{\mathtt{T}}}\to\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}
exponentially fast in time, correspondingly. As such, the last equality should be understood either as a genuine equality in the case of correct initial conditions, or as a convergence result as
t\uparrow
, with the convergence being exponentially fast, when the initial conditions are misidentified. This establishes
(b)
, which completes the proof.
∎

6.6
Proof of Theorem
3.3

Proof of Theorem
3.3
.

We proceed in a similar manner as in the proof of Theorem
3.1
. We start with the distribution

\mathbb{P}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j}\big|\mathbf{y}^{j+1},\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq J\big)=\mathbb{P}\big(\mathbf{y}^{j}\big|\mathbf{y}^{j+1},\mathbf{x}^{s},s\leq j+1\big),

where in Lemma
6.6
we showed that it is Gaussian,
\mathcal{N}_{l}(\mathbf{m}_{j+1}^{j},\mathbf{P}_{j+1}^{j})
, with mean and covariance given by (
6.29
)–(
6.30
). Looking at the covariance matrix and using the definitions of the matrices appearing in its expression (up to leading-order
O(\Delta t)
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{j+1}^{j}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{C}_{j+1}^{j}\mathbf{C}^{j}_{22}(\mathbf{C}_{j+1}^{j})^{\dagger}=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{C}_{j+1}^{j}(\mathbf{C}^{j}_{12})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\underbrace{\mathbf{E}^{j}}_{\displaystyle\mathclap{=\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})}}(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\overbrace{\mathbf{F}^{j}}^{\displaystyle\mathclap{=-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big((\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\big)+O(\Delta t)}}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\overbrace{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{j}}^{\displaystyle\mathclap{\hskip 68.28644pt=\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}-\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t}}(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\big((\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\big)\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\underbrace{(\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j}-\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j})\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}}_{\displaystyle\mathclap{=(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}}}\Delta t-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\mathbf{K}^{j})^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}+\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger})\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j})^{\dagger}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j})\Delta t

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\mathbf{R}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}})^{j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{B}^{j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2}),

which shows that up to leading-order
\mathbf{P}_{j+1}^{j}=\mathbf{B}^{j}\Delta t+O(\Delta t^{2})
. This fully accounts for the diffusion tensor appearing in (
3.7
), and as such we now need to just account for the drift term through
\mathbf{m}_{j+1}^{j}-\mathbf{y}^{j+1}
.

Using (
6.29
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{j+1}^{j}-\mathbf{y}^{j+1}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+(\mathbf{E}^{j}-\mathbf{I}_{l\times l})\mathbf{y}^{j+1}-\mathbf{E}^{j}\big((\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\mathbf{F}^{j}\big(\mathbf{x}^{j+1}-\mathbf{x}^{j}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{x},j})\Delta t\big).

We analyze each term in this expression. We already know from the proof of Theorem
2.3
(see derivations for the
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{3}
and
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{4}
terms) that

\mathbf{F}^{j}\big(\mathbf{x}^{j+1}-\mathbf{x}^{j}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{x},j})\Delta t\big)=-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\big(\mathbf{x}^{j+1}-\mathbf{x}^{j}-(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{x},j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{x},j})\Delta t\big).

As for the other terms, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-\mathbf{E}^{j}\big((\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t\big)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}-(\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}-\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\big((\mathbf{I}_{l\times l}+\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t)\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}+\mathbf{f}^{\mathbf{y},j}\Delta t\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{y}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big((\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}}\circ\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\mathbf{x}})^{j}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{G}^{\mathbf{x},j}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\text{{f}}}^{j}\Delta t
